et si $Y$ est régulier, alors il existe un voisinage ouvert $U$ de $Y$ dans $X$ tel que $X$ soit régulier aux points de $U\setmin Y$.

Pour prouver la première assertion, on peut se borner au cas où $X$ est un schéma local 

=================== PAGE IV-4 199 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 198: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 199: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 200 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{200}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item We may work affinely, with $S=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$, and $\sh{J}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{J}}$. Put $Q_m=\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{J}^m$ for $m\geq1$ and $G_r=\mathfrak{J}^r/\mathfrak{J}^{r+1}$ for $r\geq0$. The graded algebra in~\emph{a)} is flat if and only if every $G_r$ is flat. Since $Q_1=G_0$, the exact sequences $0\to G_r\to Q_{r+1}\to Q_r\to0$ for $r\geq1$ prove the equivalence of~\emph{a)} and~\emph{b)}: extensions of flat modules are flat, and the kernel of a surjection between flat modules is flat \sref[0\textsubscript{\mdseries I}]{0.6.1.2}. The Tor exact sequence associated to $0\to\mathfrak{J}^m\to\mathrm{B}\to Q_m\to0$ shows that~\emph{b)} implies~\emph{c)}. Conversely, suppose that $\mathrm{B}$ is flat over $\mathrm{A}$ and that~\emph{c)} holds. For every $\mathrm{A}$-algebra $\mathrm{A}'$, the same exact sequence and the injectivity in~\emph{c)} give $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}}(Q_m,\mathrm{A}')=0$. Taking the square-zero algebra $\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{A}\oplus\mathrm{M}$, with multiplication $(a,m)(a',m')=(aa',am'+a'm)$, for an arbitrary $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{M}$, gives $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}}(Q_m,\mathrm{M})=0$. Thus every $Q_m$ is flat, proving the conditional converse. The following conclusions are made under~\emph{a)}, or equivalently~\emph{b)}.
If we set $\sh{J}_1=\sh{J}\otimes_{\sh{O}_S}\sh{O}_{S_1}$, we then have
\[
  \bigoplus_{n\geq0}\sh{J}_1^n=\left(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\sh{J}^n\right)\otimes_{\sh{O}_S}\sh{O}_{S_1},
\]
and the assertion concerning the blown-up prescheme $X_1'$ follows immediately.
Finally, to prove that $Y'$ is $S$-flat, we may again suppose that $S$ and $X$ are affine. Put $\mathrm{C}=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{B})$. By \sref{IV.19.4.2} and \sref[II]{II.2.3.6}, every point of $Y'$ has an affine neighbourhood whose ring is $\mathrm{C}_{(t)}$, with $t$ homogeneous of degree $1$. Since $\mathrm{C}$ is flat over $\mathrm{A}$, so is the localization $\mathrm{C}_t$, and hence its degree-zero direct summand $\mathrm{C}_{(t)}$. Thus $Y'$ is $S$-flat.

  \item Again suppose that $S$ and $X$ are affine, so that $\mathrm{B}$ is a finitely presented $\mathrm{A}$-algebra and $\mathfrak{J}$ is a finitely generated ideal of $\mathrm{B}$. By \sref{IV.8.9.1}, \sref{IV.8.6.3}, and \sref{IV.11.2.9}, there exist a Noetherian subring $\mathrm{A}_0\subset\mathrm{A}$, a finite-type $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra $\mathrm{B}_0$, and a finitely generated ideal $\mathfrak{J}_0\subset\mathrm{B}_0$ such that
\[
  \mathrm{B}=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A},\qquad
  \mathfrak{J}=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B},\qquad
  \operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak J}^\bullet(\mathrm{B})=\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak{J}_0}^\bullet(\mathrm{B}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A},
\]
with $\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak{J}_0}^\bullet(\mathrm{B}_0)$ flat over $\mathrm{A}_0$. By~(i), $\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}^n=\left(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}_0^n\right)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}=\left(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}_0^n\right)\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_0}\mathrm{B}$.
The algebra $\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}_0^n$ is generated over $\mathrm{B}_0$ by the finitely generated ideal $\mathfrak{J}_0$; it is therefore of finite type, and hence of finite presentation because $\mathrm{B}_0$ is Noetherian. Thus $\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}^n$ is a finitely presented $\mathrm{B}$-algebra. Likewise, $\operatorname{Proj}(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}_0^n)\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_0)$ is of finite type \sref[II]{II.2.7.1}, hence of finite presentation, and its base change $X'\to X$ is of finite presentation.
By \sref{IV.19.4.2}, the canonical immersion $Y'\to X'$ is regular. Since $Y'$ is $S$-flat by~(i), \sref{IV.19.2.4} shows that $Y'\to X'$ is transversally regular relative to $S$ and that $X'$ is $S$-flat at the points of $Y'$, hence on an open neighbourhood of $Y'$ by \sref{IV.11.3.1}. Finally, since $\operatorname{gr}_\sh{J}^\bullet(\sh{O}_X)$ is an $S$-flat $\sh{O}_X$-algebra, \sref{IV.11.3.4} shows that $X$ is $S$-flat at the points of $Y$, and hence on a neighbourhood of $Y$ by \sref{IV.11.3.1}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[19.4.7]
\label{IV.19.4.7}
Let $g:X\to S$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation, $Y$ a closed sub-prescheme of $X$ such that the composite morphism $Y\to X\to S$ is locally of finite presentation; suppose moreover that $Y$ is S-flat and that $\sh{O}_X$ is normally flat along $Y$ \sref{IV.11.3.4}.
Then \textup{(with the notations of \sref{IV.19.4.1})}, the morphism $X'\to X$ is of finite presentation, the canonical immersion $Y'\to X'$ is transversally regular of codimension $1$ relative to $S$, $Y'$ is flat over $Y$ (and hence over $S$), and the set of points of $X$ \textup{(resp.\ $X'$)} where $X$ \textup{(resp.\ $X'$)} is $S$-flat is a neighbourhood of $Y$ \textup{(resp.\ $Y'$)}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Indeed, by hypothesis $\operatorname{gr}_\sh{J}^\bullet(\sh{O}_X)$ is a flat $(\sh{O}_X/\sh{J})$-module, and hence is $S$-flat because $Y$ is $S$-flat.
We can therefore apply the results of \sref{IV.19.4.6},~(i) and~(ii).
Since $Y'$ is $Y$-isomorphic to $\operatorname{Proj}(\operatorname{gr}_\sh{J}^\bullet(\sh{O}_X))$ \sref{IV.19.4.2}, the same argument as in \sref{IV.19.4.6} shows that $Y'$ is \emph{flat} over $Y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[19.4.8]
\label{IV.19.4.8}
Suppose the hypotheses of \sref{IV.19.4.7} satisfied and moreover that the morphism $Y'\to Y$ is smooth.
Let $x'$ be a point of $Y'$, $x$ its image in $Y$.
For $Y$ to be smooth over $S$ at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that $Y'$ be smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$; when this is so, $X'$ is then smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$.
The preceding conclusions hold in particular when $X\to S$ is locally of finite presentation and the immersion $Y\to X$ is transversally regular relative to $S$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Taking into account the compatibility of the formation of a blown-up prescheme with changes of base in the case envisaged \sref{IV.19.4.6},~(i) and of \sref{IV.17.5.1} and \sref{IV.17.7.1},

it suffices to consider the case where $S$ is the spectrum of an algebraically closed field.
But then it amounts to the same to say that an S-prescheme locally of finite type is smooth or that it is regular at this point \sref{IV.17.15.1}, hence the conclusions follow from \sref{IV.19.4.4}.

When $X\to S$ is locally of finite presentation and the canonical immersion $Y\to X$ is quasi-regular, that immersion is, by definition, of finite presentation, since the Ideal defining it is of finite type; hence the composite $Y\to X\to S$ is locally of finite presentation.
We have already seen in \sref{IV.19.4.3} that under these conditions $Y'\to Y$ is smooth; moreover, $\sh{N}_{Y/X}$ is a locally free $\sh{O}_Y$-Module and $\operatorname{gr}_\sh{J}^\bullet(\sh{O}_X)$ is isomorphic to $\mathbf{S}^\bullet_{\sh{O}_Y}(\sh{N}_{Y/X})$, hence is a flat $\sh{O}_Y$-Module.
The hypotheses of \sref{IV.19.4.7} are thus all satisfied and we can apply the conclusions of \sref{IV.19.4.8}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[19.4.9]
\label{IV.19.4.9}
Under the general hypotheses of \sref{IV.19.4.8}, suppose moreover that $Y$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x$.
Then, for every generization $x_1$ of $x$ in $X$ not belonging to $Y$, $X$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x_1$.
If $Y$ is smooth over $S$, then there exists an open neighbourhood $U$ of $Y$ in $X$ such that $X$ is smooth over $S$ at the points of $U\setmin Y$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
To prove the first assertion, we can restrict ourselves to the case where $X$ is a local scheme with closed point $x$; the hypothesis then implies that $Y$ is smooth over $S$, since every neighbourhood in $Y$ of its closed point is necessarily all of $Y$. We conclude by the same reasoning as in \sref{IV.19.4.5}, using the fact that the set of points where $X'$ is smooth over $S$ is open in $X'$.
To prove the second assertion, we can reduce to the case where $S$ is Noetherian, and $X$ of finite type over $S$, using \sref{IV.8.9.1}, \sref{IV.8.6.3}, \sref{IV.11.2.9} and \sref{IV.17.7.6}; we then conclude as in \sref{IV.19.4.5}, using the fact that the set of points where $X$ is smooth over $S$ is open in $X$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[19.4.10]
\label{IV.19.4.10}
In \sref{IV.19.4.6},~(ii), replace the hypothesis that $X$ and $Y$ are locally of finite presentation over $S$ by the hypothesis that $S$ and $X$ (hence also $Y$) are \emph{locally Noetherian}.
Then it is clear that $\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\sh{J}^n$ is an $\sh{O}_X$-Algebra of finite type, that the morphism $X'\to X$ is of finite type, and it follows again from \sref{IV.19.2.4} applied to locally Noetherian preschemes, that the immersion $Y'\to X'$ is transversally regular relatively to $S$ and that $X'$ is S-flat at the points of $Y'$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[19.4.11]
\label{IV.19.4.11}
Let $X$ be a prescheme, $\sh{E}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_X$-Module, $u:\sh{E}\to\sh{O}_X$ a homomorphism of $\sh{O}_X$-Modules, $\sh{J}=u(\sh{E})$ the quasi-coherent Ideal of $\sh{O}_X$ which is the image of $u$, and $Y$ the closed sub-prescheme of $X$ defined by $\sh{J}$. Let $X'$ be the $X$-scheme obtained by blowing up $Y$.
On the other hand, let $\mathrm{P}=\mathbf{P}(\sh{E})$ be the projective bundle over $X$ defined by $\sh{E}$ \sref[II]{II.4.1.1}, $p:\mathrm{P}\to X$ the structural morphism, $\alpha_1^{\sharp}:p^*(\sh{E})\to\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}(1)$ the canonical homomorphism \sref[II]{II.4.1.5.1} and $\sh{H}$ its kernel, so that we have an exact sequence
\[
  0\to\sh{H}\to p^*(\sh{E})\xrightarrow{\alpha_1^{\sharp}}\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}(1)\to 0.
\]
Finally, let $\sh{K}$ be the quasi-coherent Ideal of $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}$ which is the image of the restriction $v:\sh{H}\to\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}$ of $p^*(u)$, and let $Z$ be the closed sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{P}$ defined by $\sh{K}$.

\oldpage[IV-4]{202}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item The image of $X'$ by the closed immersion $j:X'\to\mathbf{P}(\sh{E})$ corresponding to the surjective homomorphism of graded $\sh{O}_X$-Algebras $\mathbf{S}_{\sh{O}_X}(\sh{E})\longrightarrow\mathbf{S}_{\sh{O}_X}(\sh{J})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\sh{J}^n$
  \sref[II]{II.3.6.2} is a closed sub-prescheme of $Z$.
  \item If $\sh{E}$ is an $\sh{O}_X$-Module locally free of rank $m$, $\sh{H}$ is an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}$-Module locally free of rank $m\setmin 1$.
  \item Suppose $X$ is locally Noetherian, that $\sh{E}$ is locally free of finite type, that the sub-prescheme $Y$ is regular, and that at every point $x\in Y$, the canonical immersion $i:Y\to X$ is regular and of codimension equal to $\operatorname{rg}_{\sh{O}_x}(\sh{E}_x)$ (which we shall express by saying that the homomorphism $u$ is regular at the point $x$ (cf.\ chap.~V)).
Then the image of $X'$ by the closed immersion $j:X'\to\mathrm{P}$ is the sub-prescheme $Z$; at all points of $X'\setmin Y'$ ($Y'$ being the inverse image of $Y$ in $X'$) the immersion $j$ is transversally regular relatively to $X$; and finally, at all points $x'$ of $Y'$, $X'$ is regular, the immersion $j$ is regular and of codimension $\operatorname{rg}_{\sh{O}_P}(\sh{H}_z)$ where $z=j(x')$ (in other words the homomorphism $v:\sh{H}\to\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}}$ is regular in $z$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
All the questions being local on $X$, we can restrict ourselves to the case where $X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is affine and $\sh{E}=\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}$, where $\mathrm{E}$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-module.
To verify~(i), we can in addition examine what happens in an affine open of $\mathrm{P}$ of the form $\mathrm{D}_+(t)$, where $t\in\mathrm{E}=\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}^1(\mathrm{E})$ \sref[II]{II.2.3.14}.
If we set $\mathrm{S}=\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{E})$, then
$\Gamma(\mathrm{D}_+(t),\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P}})=\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$ \sref[II]{II.2.4.1} and
$p^*(\sh{E})|_{\mathrm{D}_+(t)}=(\mathrm{E}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{S}_{(t)})^\sim$.
Referring to the definition of $\alpha_1^{\sharp}$ \sref[II]{II.2.6.2.2}, we see that to every element
\[
  a=\sum_i x^{(i)}\otimes\frac{x_1^{(i)}x_2^{(i)}\ldots x_n^{(i)}}{t^n}
\]
of $\mathrm{E}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$, where the $x^{(i)}$ and $x_k^{(i)}$ belong to $\mathrm{E}$, it assigns
\[
  \sum_i\frac{x^{(i)}x_1^{(i)}x_2^{(i)}\ldots x_n^{(i)}}{t^n}
  \quad\text{in }(\mathrm{S}(1))_{(t)}.
\]
To prove~(i), it is enough to show that, if this last element is zero, then the image of
\[
  a'=v(a)=\sum_i u(x^{(i)})\frac{x_1^{(i)}x_2^{(i)}\ldots x_n^{(i)}}{t^n}
\]
under the canonical homomorphism
$\mathrm{S}_{(t)}\to(u(\mathrm{E}))^n_{(t)}$ \sref[II]{II.3.6.2} is also zero.
Its image in $\mathrm{A}_{u(t)}$ is
\[
  \sum_i\frac{u(x^{(i)})u(x_1^{(i)})\ldots u(x_n^{(i)})}{u(t)^n},
\]
that is, the product of $u(t)$ and the canonical image of
$\sum_i(x^{(i)}x_1^{(i)}\ldots x_n^{(i)})/t^{n+1}$ under the algebra homomorphism extending $u:\mathrm{E}\to\mathrm{A}$ from $\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$. This proves~(i).

To establish~(ii), we may suppose that $\mathrm{E}$ is a free $\mathrm{A}$-module of rank $m$. Since $\alpha_1^{\sharp}:\mathrm{E}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{S}_{(t)}\to(\mathrm{S}(1))_{(t)}$ is surjective and $(\mathrm{S}(1))_{(t)}$ is a free $\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$-module of rank $1$, the exact sequence
\[
  0\to\mathrm{H}_{(t)}\to\mathrm{E}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{S}_{(t)}\xrightarrow{\alpha_1^{\sharp}}(\mathrm{S}(1))_{(t)}\to 0
\]
splits, and $\mathrm{H}_{(t)}$ is therefore a projective $\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$-module of rank $m\setmin 1$, whence~(ii).

To prove~(iii), let us examine first what happens above a point $x\in X\setmin Y$.
The homomorphism $u:\sh{E}\to\sh{O}_X$, restricted to a neighbourhood of $x$, is then surjective, so we may restrict to the case $\sh{J}=\sh{O}_X$, in which $X'$ is canonically identified with $X$.
To see that the closed sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{P}$ which is the image of $X'$ is then identical with $Z$, we may, as at the beginning, suppose $X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ and $\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{A}^m$, so that $\mathrm{S}=\mathrm{A}[\mathrm{T}_0,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_{m-1}]$.
With the notation above we may suppose $u(t)\ne0$; the elements $t\in\mathrm{E}$ having this property generate the symmetric algebra $\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{E})$.
After changing the basis of $\mathrm{E}$ if necessary, we may therefore suppose $t=\mathrm{T}_0$ and $u(\mathrm{T}_i)=0$ for $1\leq i\leq m\setmin 1$, so that $\mathrm{S}_{(t)}$ is canonically identified with $\mathrm{A}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_{m-1}]$.
The elements of $\mathrm{H}$ are then those of the form
\[
  \sum_\alpha \mathrm{L}_\alpha(\mathbf{T})\otimes\mathbf{T}^\alpha
\]
in $\mathrm{E}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_{m-1}]$, where
$\mathrm{L}_\alpha(\mathbf{T})=\lambda_{\alpha0}+\lambda_{\alpha1}\mathrm{T}_1+\ldots+\lambda_{\alpha,m-1}\mathrm{T}_{m-1}$ and
$\sum_\alpha\mathrm{L}_\alpha(\mathbf{T})\mathbf{T}^\alpha=0$.
One checks readily that, subject to $\lambda_{00}=0$, the values of $\lambda_{\alpha0}$ may be chosen \emph{arbitrarily} for $|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\geq1$, after which the $\lambda_{\alpha i}$ for $i\geq1$ may be chosen so as to satisfy the displayed relation.
The corresponding elements of the Ideal $\sh{K}$ are therefore precisely the polynomials
$\sum_\alpha\lambda_{\alpha0}\mathbf{T}^\alpha$ \emph{without constant term}.
Since $(\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}))_{(t)}$ is identified with $\mathrm{A}$, these polynomials are exactly the kernel of
$(\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{E}))_{(t)}\to(\mathbf{S}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}))_{(t)}$.
We have thus proved, by \sref{IV.19.2.1}, that $j:X'\to\mathrm{P}$ is \emph{transversally regular} relative to $X$ at every point of $X'\setmin Y'$ and that $j(X')=Z$ at those points.
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 203 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{203}
It remains to examine what happens above a point $x\in Y$.
The regularity hypothesis on $u$ at $x$ in~(iii) is equivalent, by \sref{IV.19.1.1} and \sref[0]{0.17.1.7}, to saying that $X$ is regular at $x$ and that
\[
  u_x\otimes1:\sh{E}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{m}_x/\mathfrak{m}_x^2
\]
is injective.
Since $p:\mathrm{P}\to X$ is smooth \sref{IV.17.3.9}, for every $z\in\mathrm{P}$ above $x$, $\mathrm{P}$ is regular at $z$ \sref{IV.17.5.8}; moreover, by \sref[0]{0.17.3.3}, the canonical homomorphism
\[
  (\mathfrak{m}_x/\mathfrak{m}_x^2)\otimes_{\kres(x)}\boldsymbol{k}(z)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{m}_z/\mathfrak{m}_z^2
  \tag{19.4.11.1}\label{IV.19.4.11.1}
\]
is injective.
It follows that the homomorphism
\[
  (\sh{E}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P},z})\otimes_{\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P},z}}\boldsymbol{k}(z)
  \longrightarrow\mathfrak{m}_z/\mathfrak{m}_z^2
\]
induced by $p^*(u)$ is also injective, since it is the composite
\[
  (\sh{E}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x))\otimes_{\kres(x)}\boldsymbol{k}(z)
  \longrightarrow(\mathfrak{m}_x/\mathfrak{m}_x^2)\otimes_{\kres(x)}\boldsymbol{k}(z)
  \longrightarrow\mathfrak{m}_z/\mathfrak{m}_z^2,
\]
where the first arrow is injective by flatness and the second is the injective homomorphism \sref{IV.19.4.11.1}.
Since $\sh{H}$ is, in a neighbourhood of $z$ in $\mathrm{P}$, a direct factor of $p^*(\sh{E})$, the homomorphism
$v_z\otimes1:\sh{H}_z\otimes_{\sh{O}_{\mathrm{P},z}}\boldsymbol{k}(z)\to\mathfrak{m}_z/\mathfrak{m}_z^2$ is also injective; this establishes the regularity of $v$ at $z$.
It remains to see that the image by $j$ of $X'$ is identical to the closed sub-prescheme $Z$.
It will suffice for this to show that the image by $j$ of the points of $q^{-1}(x)$ (where $q:X'\to X$ is the structural morphism) are exactly those of $Z\cap p^{-1}(x)$ and that on the other hand at each of these points $z=j(x')$, the surjective homomorphism $\sh{O}_{Z,z}\to\sh{O}_{X',x'}$ is bijective.
By the hypothesis and \sref{IV.19.4.3}, the restriction $Y'\to Y$ of $q$ is smooth; since $\sh{O}_{Y,x}$ is regular, so is $\sh{O}_{Y',x'}$ \sref{IV.17.5.8}. Moreover, $Y'\to X'$ is a regular immersion \sref{IV.19.4.2}, and hence $\sh{O}_{X',x'}$ is regular \sref{IV.19.1.1}. The regularity of $v$ and of $\mathrm{P}$ at $z$ similarly shows that $\sh{O}_{Z,z}$ is regular.
To prove that $\sh{O}_{Z,z}\to\sh{O}_{X',x'}$ is bijective, it therefore suffices to show that the dimensions

By \sref[0]{0.17.3.3}, this is equivalent to equality of the dimensions of
$\sh{O}_{X',x'}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ and
$\sh{O}_{Z,z}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$.
We may therefore replace $X'$ and $Z$ by the fibres $q^{-1}(x)$ and $Z\cap p^{-1}(x)$ respectively, and reduce to proving that the morphism
$q^{-1}(x)\to Z\cap p^{-1}(x)$ induced by $j$ is an isomorphism.
We may now suppose that $X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$, where $\mathrm{B}=\sh{O}_{X,x}$, and that $\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{B}^m$, so that $\mathrm{S}=\mathrm{B}[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_m]$.
The hypothesis means that there is a regular system of parameters $(t_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}$ of $\mathrm{B}$ such that $u(\mathrm{T}_i)=t_i$ for $1\leq i\leq m$ \sref[0]{0.17.1.7}.
Let $\mathfrak{J}$ be the image of $u$, and let $\mathfrak{m}$ be the maximal ideal of $\mathrm{B}$. Then $\mathfrak{J}^n\otimes_\mathrm{B}(\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{m})
=\mathfrak{J}^n/\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{J}^n
=(\mathfrak{J}^n/\mathfrak{J}^{n+1})\otimes_\mathrm{B}(\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{m})$,
and consequently, by \sref{IV.16.9.4.1},
$(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}\mathfrak{J}^n)\otimes_\mathrm{B}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is identified with
$\boldsymbol{k}(x)[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_m]$.
It follows that the immersion $q^{-1}(x)\to p^{-1}(x)$ is here an \emph{isomorphism}; this implies \emph{a fortiori} that the closed sub-prescheme $Z\cap p^{-1}(x)$ is identical with the fibre $p^{-1}(x)$, and completes the proof of \sref{IV.19.4.11}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Criteria for M-regularity}
\label{subsection:IV.19.5}

We resume here, in completed form, the criteria for a sequence of elements of a ring $\mathrm{A}$ to be M-regular or M-quasi-regular, where $\mathrm{M}$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-module, already studied in \sref[0]{0.15.1}.

\begin{theorem}[19.5.1]
\label{IV.19.5.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathbf{f}=(f_i)_{1\leq i\leq n}$ a sequence of elements of $\mathrm{A}$, and $\mathrm{M}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-module. Consider the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm a)] The sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is M-regular.
\item[\rm b)] $\mathrm{H}_i(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})=0$ for every $i>0$ \sref[III]{III.1.1.3}.
\item[\rm b')] $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})=0$.
\item[\rm c)] The sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is M-quasi-regular.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the implications
\[
  \text{a)}\Longrightarrow\text{b)}\Longrightarrow\text{b')}
  \qquad\text{and}\qquad
  \text{a)}\Longrightarrow\text{c)}.
\]
Put $\mathfrak{J}=\sum_{i=1}^n f_i\mathrm{A}$.
If the modules $\mathrm{M}_i=\mathrm{M}/(\sum_{j=1}^i f_j\mathrm{M})$ are separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology (respectively, if every quotient module of a submodule of $\mathrm{M}$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology), then we also have the implication \textup{c)}$\Rightarrow$\textup{a)} \textup{(respectively, b$'$)}$\Rightarrow$\textup{a))}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The implications \emph{a)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{c)}, and \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{a)} when the $\mathrm{M}_i$ are separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology, were already proved in \sref[0]{0.15.1.9} and are recalled only for completeness.
We have also shown in \sref[III]{III.1.1.4} and \sref[III]{III.1.1.3.3} that \emph{a)} implies \emph{b)}, and \emph{b)} trivially implies \emph{b$'$)}.
It therefore remains to prove that, when every quotient module of a submodule of $\mathrm{M}$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology, \emph{b$'$)} implies \emph{a)}.
We argue by induction on $n$.
For $n=1$, the assertion follows immediately from the definitions, since $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})$ is precisely the kernel in $\mathrm{M}$ of multiplication $z\leadsto f_1z$ \sref[III]{III.1.1.1}, \sref[III]{III.1.1.2}.
Suppose then that $n\geq2$, and put $\mathbf{f}'=(f_i)_{1\leq i\leq n-1}$.
With the notation of \sref[III]{III.1.1.2},
$\mathrm{K}_\bullet(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})=\mathrm{K}_\bullet(f_n)\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{K}_\bullet(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$.
We therefore have \sref[III]{III.1.1.4.1} the exact sequence
\[
  0\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}_0(f_n,\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M}))
  \longrightarrow\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})
  \longrightarrow\mathrm{H}_1(f_n,\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M}))
  \longrightarrow0.
  \tag{19.5.1.1}\label{IV.19.5.1.1}
\]
----- French corrections on page IV-4 203 -----
[id 22247]
OLD: $P$ est régulier au point $z$
NEW: $P$ est \emph{régulier} au point $z$
FRENCH CONTEXT (corrected): lier} au point $x$ et que l'homomorphisme $u_x\otimes1:\mathscr E_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)\to\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ est \emph{injectif}. Cela étant, comme le morphisme $p:P\to X$ est lisse (17.3.9), pour tout $z\in P$ au-dessus de $x$, $P$ est \emph{régulier} au point $z$ (17.5.8); en outre (\textbf{0}, 17.3.3) l'homomorphisme canonique
\begin{equation}
  (\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2)\otimes_{\mathbf k(x)}\boldsymbol{k}(z)\longrightarrow\mathfrak m_z/\mathfrak m_z^2
  \tag{19.4.11.1}\label{IV.19.4.11.1}
\end{equation}
est \e

=================== PAGE IV-4 204 ===================

----- (English page IV-4 204: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 205 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{205}

The relation $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})=0$ implies therefore $\mathrm{H}_0(f_n,\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M}))=0$ and $\mathrm{H}_1(f_n,\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M}))=0$.
The first of these two relations means that we have $f_n\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})=\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$ \quad (\textbf{III},
$1.1.3.5$). Now, by definition (\textbf{III}, $1.1.1$), $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$ is isomorphic to a quotient of a submodule $\mathrm{N}$ of $\mathrm{M}^{n-1}$; taking on $\mathrm{M}^{n-1}$ the filtration of the $\mathrm{M}^j$ for $j\leqslant n-1$, on $\mathrm{N}$ the induced filtration and on $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$ the quotient filtration from that of $\mathrm{N}$, we obtain on $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$ a finite filtration whose quotients are isomorphic to quotients of submodules of $\mathrm{M}$, hence are separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology by hypothesis.
As the relation $f_n\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})=\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$ implies \emph{a fortiori} $\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})=\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})$,
we deduce from the hypothesis and the preceding remarks that $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M})=0$. The induction hypothesis proves then that the sequence $\mathbf{f}'$ is M-regular. On the other hand, the relation
$\mathrm{H}_1(f_n,\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f}',\mathrm{M}))=0$ means that $f_n$ is $(\mathrm{M}/(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}f_i\mathrm{M}))$-regular, hence the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is M-regular.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[19.5.2]
\label{IV.19.5.2}
Suppose that $\mathrm{A}$ is a Noetherian ring, that the $f_i$ $(1\leqslant i\leqslant n)$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and that $\mathrm{M}$ is a finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module. Then the four conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)}, \emph{b')}, \emph{c)} of \emph{\sref{IV.19.5.1}} are equivalent.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
We know indeed from $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},7.3.5)$ that every finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[19.5.3]
\label{IV.19.5.3}
Let
\[
  0\to\mathrm{M}'\to\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{M}''\to 0
\]
be an exact sequence of $\mathrm{A}$-modules, $\mathbf{f}=(f_i)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}$ an M-regular sequence, $\mathfrak{J}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_i\mathrm{A}$. Consider the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm a)] The sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\mathrm{M}''$-regular.
\item[\rm b)] The $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic filtration of $\mathrm{M}'$ is induced on $\mathrm{M}'$ by the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic filtration of $\mathrm{M}$ \textup{(in other words, the canonical homomorphism $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M}')\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ is \emph{injective})}.
\item[\rm c)] The canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{M}'/(\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_i\mathrm{M}')\to\mathrm{M}/(\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_i\mathrm{M})$ is injective.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the implications \emph{a)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{b)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{c)}, and \emph{a)} implies moreover that the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\mathrm{M}'$-regular.
If every quotient of a submodule of $\mathrm{M}''$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology, the conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)} and \emph{c)} are equivalent.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
It is clear that \emph{c)} is a consequence of \emph{b)}. Let us prove that under all the hypotheses of the last assertion, \emph{c)} implies \emph{a)}: since $\mathbf{f}$ is M-regular, by \sref{IV.19.5.1}, $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})=0$, whence an exact sequence, portion of the exact homology sequence
\[
  0\to\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M}'')\to\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M}')\to\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M}).
\]
Now, condition \emph{c)} expresses that the homomorphism $\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M}')\to\mathrm{H}_0(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M})$ is injective (\textbf{III}, $1.1.3.5$); it is therefore equivalent to $\mathrm{H}_1(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{M}'')=0$, whence, by virtue of the separation hypothesis and \sref{IV.19.5.1}, the fact that $\mathbf{f}$ is $\mathrm{M}''$-regular.

Let us show next that \emph{a)} implies \emph{c)}, and also that the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\mathrm{M}'$-regular, proceeding by induction on $n$. For $n=1$, $f_1$ being M-regular is also $\mathrm{M}'$-regular and property \emph{c)} is precisely the lemma \sref{IV.3.4.1.4}. For $n>1$, the induction hypothesis shows that

if we set $\mathbf{f}'=(f_1,\ldots,f_{n-1})$, the sequence $\mathbf{f}'$ is $\mathrm{M}'$-regular and, by virtue of \emph{c)} applied to $\mathbf{f}'$, we have an exact sequence
\[
  0\to\mathrm{M}'/(\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}f_i\mathrm{M}')\to\mathrm{M}/(\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}f_i\mathrm{M})\to\mathrm{M}''/(\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}f_i\mathrm{M}'')\to 0.

\[
  \operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M}')\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^0(\mathrm{M}')[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n],\qquad
  \operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^0(\mathrm{M})[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]

$(\mathbf{0},15.1.7)$, and as we saw above that $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^0(\mathrm{M}')\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^0(\mathrm{M})$ is injective, the same holds for $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M}')\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})$.

We note moreover that the equivalence of conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)}, \emph{c)} of \sref{IV.19.5.3} holds in particular when $\mathrm{A}$ \emph{is Noetherian}, $\mathrm{M}''$ \emph{is a finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-module, and the $f_i$ belong to the radical of} $\mathrm{A}$.

\begin{env}[19.5.4]
\label{IV.19.5.4}
In the rest of this number, we preserve the notations $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathbf{f}$, $\mathrm{M}$, $\mathfrak{J}$ of \sref{IV.19.5.1}, and we suppose moreover $\mathrm{M}$ endowed with a decreasing filtration $(\mathrm{M}_k)$ formed of sub-$\mathrm{A}$-modules, such that $\mathrm{M}_0=\mathrm{M}$. Recall $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5)$ that we then define on $\mathrm{M}$ a second decreasing filtration formed of submodules
\[
  \mathrm{M}_k'=\mathrm{M}_k+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{k-1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}^{k-1}\mathrm{M}_1+\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}_0
\]
and that, if $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ and $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M})$ are the graded $\mathrm{A}$-modules associated to the filtrations $(\mathrm{M}_k)$ and $(\mathrm{M}_k')$ respectively, we define a surjective graded homomorphism of degree $0$ $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$
\[
  \psi_\mathbf{M}:(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M})
\]
Recall also that we have the canonical surjective homomorphism $(\mathbf{0},15.1.1.1)$
\[
  \varphi_{\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})}:(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M}))

\end{env}

\begin{proof}
The implication \emph{a)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{b)} has been demonstrated in $(\mathbf{0},15.1.8)$; the implication \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{a)} under the separation hypothesis is a particular case of $(\mathbf{0},15.1.9)$. It remains to prove that \emph{b)} implies \emph{c)}, which will be done in several steps.

We denote by $\mathfrak{J}$ the ideal $\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_i\mathrm{A}$ generated by the $f_i$; for every sequence $\mathbf{q}=(q_1,\ldots,q_n)$ of $n$ integers $\geqslant 0$, we set $|\mathbf{q}|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_i$ and $\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q}=f_1^{q_1}f_2^{q_2}\ldots f_n^{q_n}$.

\begin{env}[19.5.5.1]
\label{IV.19.5.5.1}
\emph{For the map $\psi_\mathbf{M}$ to be injective (and hence bijective), it is necessary and sufficient that, for $p\geqslant 0$ and $q\geqslant 0$, the following condition be satisfied:}

$(\mathrm{I}_{q,p})$ \emph{For every family $(x_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ of elements of $\mathrm{M}_p$, the relation}
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q} x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}\mathfrak{J}^{q-i}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}+\mathrm{M}_{p+q+1}'
\]
\emph{implies} $x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$ \emph{for every $\mathbf{q}$ such that $|\mathbf{q}|=q$.}

We proceed as in $(\mathbf{0},15.1.6)$ by considering the sub-$\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{Q}_k$ (resp. $\mathrm{Q}_k'$) of the terms of degree $k$ in the first (resp. second) member of $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$. We endow $\mathrm{Q}_k$ with the filtration
\[
  (\mathrm{Q}_k)_i=\textstyle\sum_{j\leqslant k-i}\bigl(\textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{j}|=j}(\operatorname{gr}_{k-j}(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))\mathbf{T}^\mathbf{j}\bigr)
\]
and $\mathrm{Q}_k'$ the image filtration formed by $\psi_\mathbf{M}((\mathrm{Q}_k)_i)=(\mathrm{Q}_k')_i$; it suffices then to prove that the homomorphisms $(\psi_\mathbf{M})_{ki}:\operatorname{gr}_i(\mathrm{Q}_k)\to\operatorname{gr}_i(\mathrm{Q}_k')$ are injective. Now, we have
\[
  \operatorname{gr}_i(\mathrm{Q}_k)=\textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{j}|=k-i}\bigl((\mathrm{M}_i/\mathrm{M}_{i+1})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\bigr)\mathbf{T}^\mathbf{j}
  =\textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{j}|=k-i}\bigl(\mathrm{M}_i/(\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_i+\mathrm{M}_{i+1})\bigr)\mathbf{T}^\mathbf{j}
\]

on the other hand, $(\mathrm{Q}_k')_{i+1}$ is the image of $\sum_{h=0}^{k-i-1}\mathfrak{J}^{k-i-h-1}\mathrm{M}_{i+h+1}$ in $\mathrm{M}_k'/\mathrm{M}_{k+1}'$. \begin{env}[19.5.5.2]
\label{IV.19.5.5.2}
\emph{For the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ to be $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$-quasi-regular, it suffices that, for $p\geqslant 0$ and $q\geqslant 0$, the following condition be satisfied:}

$(\mathrm{S}_{q,p})$ \emph{For every family $(x_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ of elements of $\mathrm{M}_p$, the relation}
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q}x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}
\]
\emph{implies $x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$ for every $\mathbf{q}$ such that $|\mathbf{q}|=q$.}

By definition, saying that $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$-quasi-regular means that, for every $p\geqslant 0$ and every $q\geqslant 0$, the relation
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q} x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}^{q+1}\mathrm{M}_p
\]
for a family $(x_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ of elements of $\mathrm{M}_p$ implies $x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$ for every $\mathbf{q}$ such that $|\mathbf{q}|=q$. The hypothesis on the family $(x_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ means that there exists a family $(y_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ of elements of $\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$ such that $\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q}(x_\mathbf{q}^p-y_\mathbf{q}^p)\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}$; condition $(\mathrm{S}_{q,p})$ then implies $x_\mathbf{q}^p-y_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$, whence $x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_p$; hence one sees that the conditions $(\mathrm{S}_{q,p})$ imply that the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$-quasi-regular.
\end{env}

It suffices therefore to prove the following proposition:

\begin{env}[19.5.5.3]
\label{IV.19.5.5.3}
\emph{For every $r\geqslant 0$, if the conditions $(\mathrm{I}_{q,p})$ are satisfied for $p+q<r$, then the conditions $(\mathrm{S}_{q,p})$ are satisfied for $p+q<r$.}

Let us introduce the conditions

$(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$ \emph{For every family $(x_\mathbf{q}^p)_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}$ of elements of $\mathrm{M}_p$, the relation}
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q}x_\mathbf{q}^p\in\mathrm{M}_{p+1}
\]
\emph{implies}
\[
  \textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q}x_\mathbf{q}^p
  \in\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}\mathfrak{J}^{q-i}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}.
\]

It is clear that the conjunction of $(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$ and $(\mathrm{I}_{q,p})$ implies $(\mathrm{S}_{q,p})$. On the other hand, the conditions $(\mathrm{A}_{1,p})$ and $(\mathrm{S}_{0,p})$ are trivial. Consequently, \sref{IV.19.5.5.3} will follow from the following proposition:
\end{env}

\begin{env}[19.5.5.4]
\label{IV.19.5.5.4}
\emph{For all $p\geqslant 0$ and $q\geqslant 1$, the condition $(\mathrm{A}_{q+1,p})$ is implied by the conjunction of the conditions $(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$, $(\mathrm{I}_{q-1,p+1})$, $(\mathrm{I}_{q-2,p+2})$, $\ldots$, $(\mathrm{I}_{0,p+q})$.}

Indeed, once this proposition is proved, the conditions $(\mathrm{I}_{q,p})$ supposed satisfied for $p+q<r$ will imply, for each $p\leqslant r$, the condition $(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$ for $1\leqslant q<r\setmin p$, by induction on $q$.

Let us prove \sref{IV.19.5.5.4}. Let us note that the condition $(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$ is also equivalent to
\end{env}

\begin{env}[19.5.5.5]
\label{IV.19.5.5.5}
\[
  \mathfrak{J}^q\mathrm{M}_p\cap\mathrm{M}_{p+1}\subset\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}\mathfrak{J}^{q-i}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}.
\]

Let therefore $m\in\mathfrak{J}^{q+1}\mathrm{M}_p\cap\mathrm{M}_{p+1}\subset\mathfrak{J}^q\mathrm{M}_p\cap\mathrm{M}_{p+1}$. Applying condition $(\mathrm{A}_{q,p})$, we see that for each $i$ such that $1\leqslant i\leqslant q$, there exists a family $(y_\mathbf{q}^{p+i})_{|\mathbf{q}|=q-i}$ of elements of $\mathrm{M}_{p+i}$ such that
\[
  m=\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}\bigl(\textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q-i}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q} y_\mathbf{q}^{p+i}\bigr).
\]
Suppose that for $1\leqslant i<j$ ($j\geqslant 1$), we have proved that
\[
  y_\mathbf{q}^{p+i}\in\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}+\mathrm{M}_{p+i+1}\qquad(\text{for }|\mathbf{q}|=q-i).
\]
We deduce by definition \sref{IV.19.5.4}
\[
  m-\textstyle\sum_{i<j}\bigl(\textstyle\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|=q-i}\mathbf{f}^\mathbf{q} y_\mathbf{q}^{p+i}\bigr)\in\mathrm{M}_{p+q+1}',
\]
Since by hypothesis $(\mathrm{I}_{q-j,p+j})$ is satisfied, we deduce
\[
  y_\mathbf{q}^{p+j}\in\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{p+j}+\mathrm{M}_{p+j+1}
\]

\[
  \textup{a)}\Rightarrow\textup{b)}\Rightarrow\textup{c)}.

En outre, si, pour tout entier $k\geq0$, les modules quotients des sous-modules de $\operatorname{gr}_k(M)$ sont séparés pour la topologie $\mathfrak J$-préadique \textup{(ce qui est le cas lorsque $A$ est noethérien, que les $f_i$ sont dans le radical de $A$ et que les $\operatorname{gr}_k(M)$ sont des $A$-modules de type fini)}, alors les conditions \textup{a)}, \textup{b)} et \textup{c)} sont équivalentes.
\end{theorem}

----- (English page IV-4 207: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 208: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 209 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{209}
and, by induction on $j$, one sees therefore that this condition is satisfied for \emph{every} $j$ such that $1\leqslant j\leqslant q$. We have therefore
\[
  m\in\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}\mathfrak{J}^{q-i}(\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}+\mathrm{M}_{p+i+1})=\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{q+1}\mathfrak{J}^{q-i+1}\mathrm{M}_{p+i}
\]
and we have thus proved $(\mathrm{A}_{q+1,p})$ and completed the demonstration of \sref{IV.19.5.5}.
\end{env}
\end{proof}

\begin{remarks}[19.5.6]
\label{IV.19.5.6}
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] The results of this number remain valid when, instead of an $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{M}$ and elements $f_i\in\mathrm{A}$, one takes an object $\mathrm{M}$ of any abelian category, and $n$ endomorphisms $f_i$ of $\mathrm{M}$ that mutually commute; the demonstrations adapt without difficulty to this more general case. The hypothesis of separation for an $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{N}$ relative to the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology must here be replaced by the hypothesis that every sub-object of $\mathrm{N}$ contained in all the $\sum f_i^r(\mathrm{N})$ ($r$ an arbitrary integer) is necessarily zero. The same remark applies to the results of \sref{IV.19.7}.
\item[\rm(ii)] Suppose that $\mathrm{A}$ is a \emph{graded} ring in degrees $\geq0$. If $\mathrm{M}$ (resp. each $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{M})$) is a graded $\mathrm{A}$-module with degrees bounded below, and if the $f_i$ contain no homogeneous component of degree $0$, the separation hypothesis of $(\mathbf{0},15.1.9)$ is \emph{ipso facto} satisfied for $\mathrm{M}$ (resp. each $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{M})$), and one sees that in this case we have the implication \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{a)} in \sref{IV.19.5.1} (resp. \sref{IV.19.5.5}).
\item[\rm(iii)] Recall that, without the separation hypothesis, the implication \emph{c)}$\Rightarrow$\emph{b)} is no longer valid even for $n=1$ $(\mathbf{0},15.1.12,\text{ (iii)})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remarks}

\subsection{Regular sequences relative to a filtered quotient module}
\label{subsection:IV.19.6}

\begin{env}[19.6.1]
\label{IV.19.6.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathbf{f}=(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ a finite sequence of elements of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathfrak{J}=f_1\mathrm{A}+\ldots+f_n\mathrm{A}$ the ideal it generates, and consider an exact sequence of $\mathrm{A}$-modules
\[
  0\to\mathrm{R}\xrightarrow{j}\mathrm{N}\xrightarrow{p}\mathrm{M}\to 0
\]
where one supposes that $\mathrm{N}$ is endowed with a decreasing filtration $(\mathrm{N}_k)$ of sub-$\mathrm{A}$-modules, with $\mathrm{N}_0=\mathrm{N}$. We set $\mathrm{R}_k=\mathrm{R}\cap\mathrm{N}_k$ (filtration induced by $(\mathrm{N}_k)$), $\mathrm{M}_k=p(\mathrm{N}_k)$ (quotient filtration of $(\mathrm{N}_k)$) and we denote by $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$, $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ and $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ the graded $\mathrm{A}$-modules associated with these three filtrations. We set on the other hand for all $k$,
\[
  \mathrm{N}_k'=\mathrm{N}_k+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{N}_{k-1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}^{k-1}\mathrm{N}_1+\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{N}_0

so that $\mathrm{M}_k'=p(\mathrm{N}_k')=\mathrm{M}_k+\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{M}_{k-1}+\ldots+\mathfrak{J}^{k-1}\mathrm{M}_1+\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}_0$; we set on the other hand $\mathrm{R}_k'=\mathrm{R}\cap\mathrm{N}_k'$ (filtration \emph{induced} by $(\mathrm{N}_k')$), and we denote by $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N})$, $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M})$ and $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ the graded $\mathrm{A}$-modules associated with these three filtrations; we thus have a commutative diagram of exact sequences

  0 \ar[r] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R}) \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \ar[r]_-{\operatorname{gr}'(p)} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \ar[r] & 0

\begin{env}[19.6.2]
\label{IV.19.6.2}
We defined in $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$ the surjective graded homomorphisms of degree $0$
\begin{align*}
  \psi_\mathrm{N} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \\
  \psi_\mathrm{M} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \\
  \psi_\mathrm{R} &: (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \to \operatorname{gr}_\bullet''(\mathrm{R})
\end{align*}
the first two of which are deduced from the last two vertical arrows of \sref{IV.19.6.1.1}.

As the filtration $(\mathrm{R}_k'')$ is finer than $(\mathrm{R}_k')$, on $\mathrm{R}$ we have a canonical homomorphism $\gamma:\operatorname{gr}_\bullet''(\mathrm{R})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$; we will denote by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ the composite homomorphism $\gamma\circ\psi_\mathrm{R}$. It then follows immediately from the definitions that we have a commutative diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  & 0 \ar[d] \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{R}'} \ar[d]_{j'} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R}) \ar[d]^{\operatorname{gr}'(j)} \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{N}} \ar[d]_{p'} & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{N}) \ar[d]^{\operatorname{gr}'(p)} \\
  (\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n] \ar[r]^-{\psi_\mathrm{M}} \ar[d] & \operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{M}) \ar[d] \\
  0 & 0
}
\tag{19.6.2.1}
\label{IV.19.6.2.1}
\]
where the columns are exact.
\end{env}

\begin{proposition}[19.6.3]
\label{IV.19.6.3}
With the preceding notations, suppose that the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$-regular. Consider the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm a)] $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{M})$-regular.
\item[\rm b)] $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is bijective.
\item[\rm c)] $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is surjective \textup{(in other words $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ is generated as $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{J}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module by $\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R}))$)}.
\item[\rm d)] $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is injective.
\item[\rm \textup{d$'$)}] The homomorphism $(\psi_\mathrm{R}')_0:\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ obtained by restricting $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ to polynomials of degree $0$, is injective.
\item[\rm e)] $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is bijective.
\item[\rm f)] $j'$ is injective.

Puisque par hypothèse $(\mathrm I_{q-j,p+j})$ est vérifiée, on en déduit

  y_{\mathbf q}^{p+j}\in\mathfrak JM_{p+j}+M_{p+j+1}

et on a ainsi prouvé $(\mathrm A_{q+1,p})$ et terminé la démonstration de (19.5.5).

\begin{remarks}[19.5.6

=================== PAGE IV-4 210 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 209: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 210: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 211 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{211}
\item[\rm \textup{f$'$)}] The homomorphism $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$ obtained by restricting $j'$ to polynomials of degree $0$, is injective.
\item[\rm g)] The $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic filtration of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ is induced by that of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$.
\item[\rm h)] The filtrations $(\mathrm{R}_k')$ and $(\mathrm{R}_k'')$ on $\mathrm{R}$ are identical.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the following implications:
\[
\xymatrix@C=2.4em@R=1.8em{
  \textup{a)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] &
  \textup{e)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] &
  \textup{b)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \textup{c)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \textup{h)} \\
  & \textup{g)} \ar@{=>}[r] &
  \textup{d)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \textup{d$'$)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \textup{f)} \ar@{<=>}[r] &
  \textup{f$'$)}
}
\]

Moreover, when every quotient module of a submodule of a $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{M})$ is separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology \textup{(which is the case when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and the $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{M})$ are finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-modules)}, then the conditions \textup{a)} to \textup{h)} are all equivalent.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let us note that, from \sref{IV.19.5.5}, the hypothesis that $\mathbf{f}$ is $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{N})$-regular implies that $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ is \emph{bijective}; as $\operatorname{gr}'(j)$ is injective, the equivalence of \emph{d)} and \emph{f)} results from the diagram \sref{IV.19.6.2.1}. Likewise \emph{d$'$)} and \emph{f$'$)} are equivalent, while \emph{f)} and \emph{f$'$)} are trivially equivalent; this proves the equivalence of \emph{d)}, \emph{d$'$)}, \emph{f)} and \emph{f$'$)}.

As one already knows that $\psi_\mathrm{M}$ is surjective and $\psi_\mathrm{N}$ bijective, the equivalence of \emph{b)} and \emph{c)} also follows from the diagram \sref{IV.19.6.2.1} by a particular case of the five lemma.

The relation $\psi_\mathrm{R}'=\gamma\circ\psi_\mathrm{R}$ \sref{IV.19.6.2} and the fact that $\psi_\mathrm{R}$ is surjective show that condition \emph{c)} is equivalent to saying that $\gamma$ is surjective, which is also equivalent to condition \emph{h)}; hence we have proved the equivalence of \emph{b)}, \emph{c)} and \emph{h)}.

It is trivial that \emph{e)} is equivalent to the conjunction of \emph{c)} and \emph{d)}, and hence also of \emph{b)} and \emph{d)}. Let us note now that \emph{a)} implies \emph{b)} and that \emph{b)} implies \emph{a)} under the separation hypothesis \sref{IV.19.5.5}. On the other hand, the implications \emph{a)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{g)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{f$'$)}, and the implication \emph{f$'$)} $\Rightarrow$ \emph{a)} under the separation hypothesis, result from \sref{IV.19.5.3}. Hence we have proved \emph{a)} implies \emph{e)} (equivalent to the conjunction of \emph{b)} and \emph{f$'$)}), and also that all conditions are equivalent under the separation hypothesis.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[19.6.4]
\label{IV.19.6.4}
Let $\mathfrak K$ be an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{J}+\mathfrak K$. Suppose that the filtration $(\mathrm{N}_k)$ (hence also $(\mathrm{M}_k)$) is the $\mathfrak K$-preadic filtration, so that the filtrations $(\mathrm{M}_k')$ and $(\mathrm{N}_k')$ are $\mathfrak{L}$-preadic filtrations. Consider $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ \textup{(resp. $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$)} as a graded module over $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$ \textup{(resp. $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$)}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] Let $\mathrm{S}$ be a part of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ that generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module. Then condition \emph{c)} of \emph{\sref{IV.19.6.3}} is equivalent to the following condition:
\item[\rm \textup{c$'$)}] The image of $\mathrm{S}$ in $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module.
\item[\rm(ii)] Suppose condition \emph{e)} of \emph{\sref{IV.19.6.3}} is satisfied, and also that the quotients of $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{R})$ are separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology \textup{(which is the case when $\mathrm{A}$ is Noetherian, the $f_i$ belong to the radical of $\mathrm{A}$, and the $\operatorname{gr}_k(\mathrm{N})$ are finitely generated $\mathrm{A}$-modules)}. Then, for a part $\mathrm{S}$ of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ formed of homogeneous elements to generate $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that its image by $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}

  \item[\textup{f)}] $j'$ est injectif.

\oldpage[IV-4]{211}

  \item[\textup{f$'$)}] L'homomorphisme $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(R)\otimes_A(A/\mathfrak J)\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(N)\otimes_A(A/\mathfrak J)$ obtenu en restreignant $j'$ aux polynômes de degré $0$, est injectif

=================== PAGE IV-4 211 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 210: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 211: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, source_aligned/ega4-19.tex, page IV-4 212 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{212}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] Consider the homomorphism of degree $0$
\[
  \psi_\mathrm{A}:(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{A})=\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})
\]
$(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$. The fact that this homomorphism is \emph{surjective} implies that the sub-$\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ generated by $\mathrm{S}'=\psi_\mathrm{R}'(\mathrm{S})$ is none other than $\operatorname{Im}(\psi_\mathrm{R}')$, whence the equivalence of \emph{c)} and \emph{c$'$)}.

\item[\rm(ii)] Since \emph{e)} is satisfied, so is \emph{c)}, and by virtue of (i) it remains to prove the sufficiency of the condition in the statement. Now, as $\psi_\mathrm{R}'$ is bijective, to say that $\mathrm{S}'$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet'(\mathrm{R})$ considered as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module amounts to saying that $\mathrm{S}$ generates $(\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module, and by the surjectivity of $\psi_\mathrm{A}$, we can in this statement replace the ring $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$ by $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$. It amounts to saying that $\mathrm{S}$ generates $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$ as a module over the ring $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$, or also as a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module. Now, if $\mathrm{T}$ is the graded sub-$\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{A})$-module of $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})$ generated by $\mathrm{S}$, and if we set $\mathrm{T}'=\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(\mathrm{R})/\mathrm{T}$, then by hypothesis $\mathrm{T}'/\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{T}'=0$, or $\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{T}'=\mathrm{T}'$; but $\mathrm{T}'$ is a graded module, and each $\mathrm{T}_p'$ is a quotient of $\operatorname{gr}_p(\mathrm{R})$, hence separated for the $\mathfrak{J}$-preadic topology; the hypothesis $\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{T}_p'=\mathrm{T}_p'$ therefore implies $\mathrm{T}_p'=0$ for every integer $p$, hence $\mathrm{T}'=0$, which completes the proof of the corollary.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\subsection{Hironaka's normal flatness criterion}
\label{subsection:IV.19.7}
\label{IV.19.7}

\begin{theorem}[19.7.1]
\label{IV.19.7.1}
\textup{(Hironaka).}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathfrak K$ an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathbf{f}=(f_1,\ldots,f_n)$ a sequence of elements of $\mathrm{A}$ that is $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)$-regular, $\mathfrak{J}=f_1\mathrm{A}+\ldots+f_n\mathrm{A}$ the ideal generated by $\mathbf{f}$, $\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{J}+\mathfrak K$, $\mathrm{M}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-module.
In this case $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})=(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})=(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, since $(\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak K^{n+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})=\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/(\mathfrak K^{n+1}+\mathfrak{J}\mathfrak K^n)\mathrm{M}=\mathfrak K^n\mathrm{M}/(\mathfrak K^{n+1}+\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak K^n)\mathrm{M}$, and the last equality is a result of \textup{Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~II, $3^e$~ed., \textsection~3, n\textsuperscript{o}~7, cor.~2 of prop.~6}.

Consider the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm a)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)$-module.
\item[\rm b)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module and the canonical homomorphism $(\mathbf{0},15.1.5.2)$
\[
  \psi_\mathrm{M}:((\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L}))[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]\to\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})
\]
is bijective.
\item[\rm c)] $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module and the sequence $\mathbf{f}$ is $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))$-regular.
\item[\rm d)] $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak{L}^\bullet(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$-module, and for every $\mathfrak{p}\in\operatorname{Ass}_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{L})$, $(\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^\bullet(\mathrm{M}))_\mathfrak{p}$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak K)_\mathfrak{p}$-module.
\end{enumerate}
Then we have the implications
\[
\xymatrix@C=3em@R=1.6em{
  \textup{a)} \ar@{=>}[r] \ar@{=>}[dr] & \textup{c)} \ar@{=>}[r] & \textup{b)} \\
  & \textup{d)}
}
\]

When every quotient of a submodule of a $\operatorname{gr}_\mathfrak K^p(\mathrm{M})$ $(p\geqslant 0)$ is ideally separated for $\mathfrak{J}$ \textup{(Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.~comm.}, chap.~III, \textsection~5, n\textsuperscript{o}~1)}, the conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{b)} and \emph{c)} are equivalent.

  \item[\textup{g)}] La filtration $\mathfrak J$-préadique de $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(R)$ est induite par celle de $\operatorname{gr}_\bullet(N)$.
  \item[\textup{h)}] Les filtrations $(R_k')$ et $(R_k'')$ sur $R$ sont identiques.
\end{enumerate}

\end{proposition}
